| L4 `setfenv(0, t)` in addon code changes the running thread's environment: `getfenv(0)` then returns `t` and `loadstring` gives new chunks `t`; `setfenv(1, t)` changes the calling function's. Kit choice: the thread environment addon code sets is kept per world, not per coroutine, and never becomes the host thread's, so kit code loaded meanwhile keeps the real `_G` | confirmed (Lua 5.1); assumed (kit choice: one per world) | Lua 5.1 reference manual `setfenv`: "As a special case, when f is 0 setfenv changes the environment of the running thread."; a chunk `loadstring` compiles gets the thread's environment (Lua 5.1 `ldo.c` `f_parser`: `luaF_newLclosure(L, tf->nups, hvalue(gt(L)))`); wiki `API:setfenv`. Known gap: the client would load the files of addons loaded afterwards with that environment too (inferred: it loads them on the same thread), where the kit keeps loading them in the one `_G`. Known gap: misuse messages always name `setfenv`, as for `setmetatable` (L2). The client's API functions are C functions, which `setfenv` refuses with `'setfenv' cannot change environment of given object` (Lua 5.1 `lbaselib.c` `luaB_setfenv`). Kit choice: `setfenv` refuses with that message every function whose environment is the host's `_G`, named directly or by level: every kit function, Blizzard-fake FrameXML functions included, which are Lua and so changeable in the client. Known gap: a level-1 `setfenv` in tail position (`return setfenv(1, t)`) raises `'setfenv' cannot reach the calling function: a tail call (return setfenv(...)) replaced it`, where Lua 5.1 changes the function's environment and returns it; the kit cannot reach the replaced function. Functions a test installs with `w:setGlobal` have the host's `_G` as their environment too, so they count as kit functions and `setfenv` refuses them. Known gap: the kit calls addon code through a tail call (`world_events.lua` `methods:try`), so level 2 is a lost frame in every entry the kit dispatches: a file's main chunk, script handlers and script hooks, `hooksecurefunc` hooks, `securecall` functions, `C_Timer.NewTimer`/`NewTicker` callbacks, slash commands and menu callbacks. `setfenv(2, t)` there raises `no function environment for tail call at level 2`, where the client's level 2 would be its C caller and the message the refusal above (inferred: the client dispatches these from C; no source shows its stack) (`C_Timer.After` wraps its callback in a kit function, so it gets the refusal) (`tests/test_addon_fenv_errors.lua`). Known gap: as a coroutine body, `coroutine.wrap(setfenv)` and `coroutine.create(setfenv)` are accepted and the call raises `bad argument #1 to 'setfenv' (invalid level)`, where Lua 5.1 rejects them at the caller's line with `bad argument #1 to 'wrap'` (or `'create'`) `(Lua function expected)` (`'?'` under `pcall`): the kit's `setfenv` is a Lua function |
| L5 each `SavedVariables` / `SavedVariablesPerCharacter` name is one global, dots included (`My.DB` is `_G["My.DB"]`, not `My.DB`) | assumed (no page says what the client does with a dot in the name) | wiki `TOC_format`: `SavedVariables` are "Global variables to be stored in and read from `WTF\[account]\SavedVariables\MyAddOn.lua`", `SavedVariablesMachine` a "List of global variable names"; no page names path splitting, as for `CreateFrame` names (F2). `Addon.applySaved` sets them with `w:setGlobal`, which no longer splits dotted names (`w:install` still does) |
| L6 the Forever client answers to the game types `forever` and `camelot` (and the family `mainline`) and reads `<Addon>_Forever.toc` or `<Addon>_Camelot.toc` before `<Addon>_Mainline.toc`. Kit choices: the kit counts `forever` as known on every flavor (L1), so a `forever`-only file loads on no other flavor; with both TOCs present, `_Camelot` wins | confirmed (game types, suffixes, family priority); assumed (kit choices: `forever` known everywhere, the order of `_Camelot` and `_Forever`) | wiki `TOC_format` (`Template:GameTypes`): game type "`forever` / `camelot`", suffixes "`AddOnName_Forever.toc` / `AddOnName_Camelot.toc`", family `mainline` = "`standard`, `forever`, `camelot`, `plunderstorm`, `wowhack`"; "Family suffixes have a lower priority"; Forever patch 1.60.1: "Added Camelot game type, Game variable value and TOC suffix, with Forever game type and TOC suffix alias." No page orders the two alias suffixes; the kit tries `camelot`, the game type, before its alias |
| L7 the client skips a UTF-8 BOM at the start of an addon Lua file; a BOM anywhere else stays a syntax error (assumed) | confirmed (extract) | Blizzard FrameXML, local extract retail build 70575, code/addons/blizzard_framexmlbase/constants.lua:1 (BOM) listed at blizzard_framexmlbase.toc:17; also blizzard_charactercustomize.lua, blizzard_communities/clubfinderapplicantlist.lua |

## GameTooltip

Retail paths are relative to `mirror/gethe/live/` (read on GitHub `Gethe/wow-ui-source` branch `live`, 2026-10-20).

| Rule | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|
| T1 `SetUnit` on a unit that does not exist leaves the tooltip empty and `GetUnit()` returning nothing, even after an earlier unit was shown | assumed (no Lua shows the engine side) | wiki `API_GameTooltip_GetUnit`: "Returns nil when the tooltip is not shown, or when showing something other than a unit." (pages for mists, tbc, vanilla); `API_GameTooltip_SetUnit` says nothing about a missing unit. Retail: `GameTooltipDataMixin:GetUnit` (`Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_GameTooltip/Mainline/GameTooltip.lua:1035-1037`) calls `TooltipUtil.GetDisplayedUnit` (`Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_SharedXMLGame/Tooltip/TooltipUtil.lua:34-41`), which derives the unit token from the tooltip data's `guid` (`UnitTokenFromGUID`), never from the token passed to `SetUnit` |
| T2 `GetUnit()` returns `name, unit, guid` on retail and forever, with `unit = UnitTokenFromGUID(guid)` and `name = UnitName(unit)`; on Classic flavors it returns `name, unit` | confirmed | Mainline: `GameTooltipDataMixin:GetUnit` (`Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_GameTooltip/Mainline/GameTooltip.lua:1035-1037`; `forever` `:1044-1046`) returns `TooltipUtil.GetDisplayedUnit(self)` (`Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_SharedXMLGame/Tooltip/TooltipUtil.lua:34-41`, same lines on `forever`): `local unit = guid and UnitTokenFromGUID(guid); local name = unit and UnitName(unit); return name, unit, guid;`. `classic` has no Lua `GetUnit` (`Blizzard_GameTooltip/Classic/GameTooltip.lua`), so it is the C method: wiki `API_GameTooltip_GetUnit` (Mists, TBC, Vanilla pages): `unitName, unitId = GameTooltip:GetUnit()`. Token order: wiki `API_UnitTokenFromGUID`, "Unit tokens will be tested in the order given below, returning the first one that matches": `player`, `vehicle`, `pet`, `party<x>`, `partypet<x>`, `raid<x>`, `raidpet<x>`, `nameplate<x>`, `arena<x>`, `arenapet<x>`, `boss<x>`, `target`, `focus`, `npc`, `mouseover`, `softenemy`, `softfriend`, `softinteract`. The kit's `UnitTokenFromGUID` (`units.lua`) tries its fixture tokens in that order, a numbered group by index, and never returns a token outside the list (`targettarget`) |
| T3 on retail and forever, hiding the tooltip forgets its unit: `GetUnit()` then returns nothing. On Classic flavors the tooltip's own `Hide` or `SetShown(false)` taking it from shown to hidden forgets the unit, and so does an ancestor's hide (`UIParent:Hide()`) that takes a visible tooltip to not visible, whatever its scripts; a tooltip not shown since `SetUnit` still answers | confirmed (retail, forever); assumed (Classic: user decision, 2026-10-20) | `GameTooltipTemplate` has `<OnHide function="GameTooltip_OnHide"/>` (`Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_GameTooltip/Mainline/GameTooltip.xml:27`, same on `forever`; `GameTooltip` inherits it, `:249`), and `GameTooltip_OnHide` calls `self:ClearHandlerInfo()` (`GameTooltip.lua:421`; `forever` `:430`), after which `TooltipUtil.GetDisplayedUnit`'s `IsTooltipType` check is inferred to fail. The kit sets that `OnHide` on `GameTooltip`, so an addon's `SetScript("OnHide", ...)` replaces it as in the client. Classic: wiki `API_GameTooltip_GetUnit` says "Returns nil when the tooltip is not shown", but the kit's `SetUnit` does not show the tooltip, and whether the client's does (so `GetUnit` works inside `OnTooltipSetUnit`) is unsourced; the wiki's `SetUnit` example calls `Show()` after it. Verdict (user): forget the unit only on a shown-to-hidden change, which every reading agrees on, so `GetUnit` inside `OnTooltipSetUnit` keeps working. Classic's `GetUnit` is C, so an addon's `OnHide` script does not change this (`framexml/fakes/tooltip_unit.lua`). An ancestor's hide counts as a hide (assumed, same user decision): the kit fires `OnHide` on every visible descendant when a frame hides (`ui/behaviour.lua` `fireVisibility`), so on retail and forever it already ran `GameTooltip_OnHide`, and on Classic the kit forgets the unit right after the tooltip's `OnHide` scripts. A tooltip made with `CreateFrame("GameTooltip", name, UIParent, "GameTooltipTemplate")` gets the same `OnHide`: the template's own `<OnHide function="GameTooltip_OnHide"/>` (`GameTooltip.xml:27`, template `:4-37`, read on GitHub at the pinned `live` and `forever` commits, identical; the mirror does not hold `Blizzard_GameTooltip`) replaces `SharedTooltipTemplate`'s appended `SharedTooltip_OnHide` (`Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_SharedXML/SharedTooltipTemplates.xml:98`), and the kit's `GameTooltip_OnHide` global is that handler for both. Known gap: on Classic flavors the kit defines no `GameTooltip_OnHide`, so a template tooltip's `GetScript("OnHide")` is nil there; its unit is forgotten by the Classic rule above all the same. Known gap: the template's `TextLeft1`, `TextRight1`, `TextLeft2`, `TextRight2` keys are not made (`ui/templates.lua`) |

## Layout

Wiki pages read 2026-10-24 (`warcraft.wiki.gg`; `API_Region_SetPoint` redirects to `API:ScriptRegionResizing_SetPoint`, and `API_ScriptRegion_SetPoint` is a 404 as planned). Lua paths are relative to `mirror/gethe/live/Interface/AddOns/` unless a row says `classic_era`; `api/<flavor>/` is the kit's generated docs.

| Rule | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|
| LY1 `SetPoint` with `relativeTo` omitted anchors to the parent, else to the screen; omitted `relativePoint` is `point`; omitted offsets are 0 | confirmed (wiki). Edit Mode exception not modelled | wiki `API_Region_SetPoint` (`ScriptRegionResizing:SetPoint`): "If omitted, anchors to the parent region if applicable, otherwise to the screen dimensions. Exceptions to this rule are frames managed by Edit Mode."; `relativePoint`: "If omitted, defaults to `point`."; `offsetX`/`offsetY` are `number?` with `default=0`, each "Requires `offsetY`/`offsetX` to be specified". Its overload example calls `f:SetPoint("BOTTOMLEFT")`, `("BOTTOMLEFT", 0, 0)`, `("BOTTOMLEFT", UIParent)` and so on on a frame made with parent `UIParent` and says "all of the calls have the same result". A parentless frame anchors to the screen: the anchor family example makes `CreateFrame("Frame")` (no parent), sets `TOPLEFT` on `UIParent`, then `f:SetPoint("BOTTOMRIGHT")` with the comment "anchors between UIParent and the screen dimensions" |
| LY2 an explicit `nil` `relativeTo` anchors to the screen, even for a frame with a parent | confirmed (wiki); spec §4 differs: nil means the parent | wiki `API_Region_SetPoint`: "If explicitly passed `nil`, anchors relative to the screen dimensions." (same page and entry as LY1, directly under the omitted-argument sentence). Spec §4 says a nil `relativeTo` means the parent; no Lua in the mirror calls `SetPoint(point, nil, ...)` to settle it |
| LY3 the screen anchor target is the physical screen rect (0,0)-(width,height), seen from the region's own scale | assumed (no source states the rect or its scale space) | no source: wiki `API_Region_SetPoint` says only "the screen dimensions"; wiki `API_GetScreenWidth` says the width is "measured at UIParent scale", not the physical size |
| LY4 a string `relativeTo` resolves through `_G`; a string naming no global, or a global that holds no table (a string), anchors to the screen like `nil`; `SetParent`, `SetFontObject` and the button `Set*FontObject` setters read such a name as missing too | confirmed (string form); assumed (missing global, non-table global) | wiki `API_Region_SetPoint`: `relativeTo` is `ScriptRegion,string?`, "For example `UIParent` or `"UIParent"`."; example `f:SetPoint("BOTTOMLEFT", "UIParent", "BOTTOMLEFT")` has the same result as the `UIParent` forms. The page does not say what a string with no matching global does |
| LY5 two opposite anchors on an axis set the size on it and beat an explicit size; one anchor plus an explicit size places the region; too few constraints give an invalid rect | confirmed (an inferred size beats `SetSize`); assumed (one anchor plus a size, and the invalid-rect case) | wiki `API_Region_SetWidth` (`ScriptRegionResizing:SetWidth`): "If the width/height is inferred from anchor points, then setting it has no effect." (its example sets `f:SetAllPoints()` and a later `f:SetSize(100, 100)` leaves the size unchanged). The pages say nothing on one anchor plus a size or on too few constraints beyond LY6 |
| LY6 an invalid rect (no resolvable anchors) makes `GetLeft`, `GetRight`, `GetTop`, `GetBottom`, `GetCenter`, `GetRect` and `GetScaledRect` return nothing; `IsRectValid` returns false | confirmed (the getters return nothing); assumed (`IsRectValid` false) | wiki `API_ScriptRegion_GetRect`: "Returns `nil` if the region has no points."; `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleScriptRegionAPIDocumentation.lua:169` (`GetRect`) and `:215` (`GetScaledRect`) carry `MayReturnNothing = true`; `api/retail/SimpleScriptRegionAPI.lua` has `mayReturnNothing = true` on `GetBottom` (`:112`), `GetCenter` (`:127`), `GetLeft` (`:173`), `GetRect` (`:188`), `GetRight` (`:221`), `GetScaledRect` (`:236`), `GetTop` (`:343`). `IsRectValid` is documented only as returning `isValid: bool` (`SimpleScriptRegionAPIDocumentation.lua:531`, `api/retail/SimpleScriptRegionAPI.lua:650`); that it is false for a region with no points is read off the name and the wiki `SetPoint` page's "See also: ScriptRegion:IsRectValid" |
| LY7 offsets are in the region's own scale space; effective scale is the product of scales up the parent chain; `GetRect` is in the region's units; `GetScaledRect` is `GetRect` times the scale | confirmed (offsets scale, effective scale product, the `GetRect` example); assumed (`GetScaledRect` uses the effective rather than the own scale) | wiki `API_Region_SetPoint`, Details: "UI scaling affects the distances for offsetX and offsetY; increasing the Region's scale will increase the offset."; wiki `API_Region_GetEffectiveScale`: "The effective scale after propagating from the parent region. This is equal to `f:GetScale() * f:GetParent():GetScale()`", with `parent:SetScale(0.5)`, `f:SetScale(0.5)` giving `0.25` and `f:SetIgnoreParentScale(true)` giving `0.5`; wiki `API_ScriptRegion_GetRect`: "`GetScaledRect()` is the result of multiplying by [GetScale]", example `f:SetPoint("BOTTOMLEFT", 100, 100)`, `f:SetSize(25, 25)`, `f:SetScale(2)` gives `print(f:GetRect()) -- 100, 100, 25, 25` and `print(f:GetScaledRect()) -- 200, 200, 50, 50`. The example frame has no parent, so own and effective scale coincide in it |
| LY8 `GetScreenWidth`/`GetScreenHeight` are UIParent's size in UI units; `GetPhysicalScreenSize` is the physical size; UIParent size = physical ÷ UI scale, with UI scale 1 while `useUiScale` is off and `uiScale` when on. `UIParent_GetEffectiveScale` is not installed | confirmed (the two getters, the physical size, no `UIParent_GetEffectiveScale`); assumed (the UI scale rule) | wiki `API_GetScreenWidth`: "Returns the width of the window measured at UIParent scale (effectively the same as UIParent:GetWidth()", its example comment "the return value does not match the screen resolution set in the Video Options, but will instead represent a dimension with the same aspect ratio" and `GetScreenWidth() * UIParent:GetEffectiveScale()` for pixels (`API_GetScreenHeight` is the same); wiki `API_GetPhysicalScreenSize`: "Returns physical screen size of game." with "game physical screen width"/"height"; `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/ScreenDocumentation.lua:19` (`GetPhysicalScreenSize`, two non-nilable numbers; `GetScreenWidth` `:47`, `GetScreenHeight` `:38`; `classic_era` has no `GetScreenWidth` in its docs). UI scale: wiki `CVar_useUiScale`: "This feature is used to enable/disable the use of scaling down the User Interface."; wiki `CVar_uiScale`: sets the UI scale for UIParent, "Requires setting useUiScale to 1", values below 0.64 do nothing, takes effect at the next login. `Blizzard_SharedXML/Mainline/UIDropDownMenu.lua:1075-1083` reads `GetCVar("useUIScale") == "1"` then `tonumber(GetCVar("uiscale"))`, else `UIParent:GetScale()`; no source says the scale is 1 while the CVar is off, and the `GetScreenWidth` example comment suggests UIParent's size need not equal the resolution. `grep -rn UIParent_GetEffectiveScale` over every mirror flavor, `api/` and the repo (outside plans and specs) finds nothing |
| LY9 an anchor that would close a cycle raises `Action[SetPoint] failed because[Cannot anchor to a region dependent on it]: attempted from: <debug name>:SetPoint.`; anchoring a region to itself raises `Action[SetPoint] failed because[Cannot anchor to itself]: attempted from: <debug name>:SetPoint.`. The `anchor family connection` text is a different error | confirmed (client error logs, not a wiki or Blizzard page); contradicts the plan default (cycle raises `anchor family connection`) | Client logs of real errors: `Action[SetPoint] failed because[Cannot anchor to a region dependent on it]: attempted from: EJMicroButton:SetPoint.` (Bartender4 issue 2278, Cataclysm Classic, 2024-11-03, wowace.com/projects/bartender4/issues/2278); `...: attempted from: AdiBagsSection15:SetPoint.` (AdiBags issue 680, github.com/AdiAddons/AdiBags/issues/680, 2022-09-01); `...: attempted from: DebuffFrame:SetPoint.` followed by lines `Relative: [BuffFrame UIParent, BuffFrame]` and `Dependent: [BuffFrame UIParent, BuffFrame]` (Blizzard forum, us.forums.blizzard.com/en/wow/t/whats-causing-this-error/2294289, 2026-04-20; newer builds add those `Relative:`/`Dependent:` lines, and EllesmereUI issue 809 also shows `Dependent ancestors:`). Self-anchor: `Action[SetPoint] failed because[Cannot anchor to itself]: attempted from: EssentialCooldownViewer:SetPoint.` (Blizzard forum, eu.forums.blizzard.com/en/wow/t/managed-to-anchor-my-essentialcooldownviewer-to-itself/603198, 2026-01-24). Anchor family: wiki `API_Region_SetPoint`, "Anchor family": "There appear to be multiple causes that can throw anchor family connection errors."; its examples are a frame anchored both to `UIParent` and to the screen, and two nameplates (patch 8.2.0: frames anchored to restricted frames can only be anchored within the same anchor family); the page says nothing on plain cycles. The mirror's Lua has no such text (`grep -rniE` over `live` and `forever` finds none) because the errors come from the C side. Spec §4 allows "Cannot anchor to itself" or "would cause a circular dependency; exact text from the sources" |
| LY10 a FontString with no explicit width is as wide as its text and, with no explicit height, as tall as its (wrapped) text; word wrap is on by default; empty text is one line tall; with no anchors `GetWidth`/`GetHeight` still report the text size | assumed (no source states the sizing rule) | no source: wiki `API_FontString_GetStringHeight`: "Returns how tall the string would be, in pixels, without wrapping:", "The height of the text will take into account manually set line breaks", "This does not necessarily correlate with the height of the FontString object"; it does not mention empty text. Wiki `API_FontString_SetWordWrap` is the signature `FontString:SetWordWrap(wrap)` with `wrap: boolean` and no default or description; wiki `UIOBJECT_FontString` says nothing on how a size is derived |
| LY11 `SetHitRectInsets(left, right, top, bottom)`: positive values shrink the mouse-sensitive rect, negative values grow it; `IsMouseOver(offsetTop, offsetBottom, offsetLeft, offsetRight)` extends the tested rect by the offsets: positive `offsetTop` and `offsetRight` grow it up and right, negative `offsetBottom` and `offsetLeft` grow it down and left | confirmed (argument order, the signs of both functions); assumed (that `IsMouseOver` tests the hit rect rather than the plain rect) | wiki `API_Frame_SetHitRectInsets`: "Sets the insets of the frame's hit rectangle.", "negative values of `left, right, top, buttom` increase the size of the frame's hit rectangle", "positive values decrease the size."; `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleFrameAPIDocumentation.lua:1313` (`left`, `right`, `top`, `bottom`). Wiki `API_ScriptRegion_IsMouseOver`: `offsetTop` "positive values extend the region for which this function returns true upward", `offsetBottom` "negative values extend ... downward", `offsetLeft` "negative values extend ... towards the left", `offsetRight` "positive values extend ... towards the right"; example `button:IsMouseOver(2, -2, -2, 2)` ("within 2 pixels of the region"); argument order `SimpleScriptRegionAPIDocumentation.lua:483` (`offsetTop`, `offsetBottom`, `offsetLeft`, `offsetRight`, default 0). The page says "the region", not the hit rect |
| LY12 the topmost frame under a point: higher strata first, then higher frame level, then later creation | confirmed (strata order, higher level in front); assumed (that hit order follows draw order, and the creation tie-break) | wiki `Frame_Strata`: strata lowest to highest `WORLD`, `BACKGROUND`, `LOW`, `MEDIUM`, `HIGH`, `DIALOG`, `FULLSCREEN`, `FULLSCREEN_DIALOG`, `TOOLTIP`; "Higher numbers appear above lower ones, but only if they share the same strata."; wiki `API_Frame_SetFrameLevel`: "Frames with higher frame level are layered "in front of" frames with a lower frame level." and "By default, every frame is drawn just in front of its parent (if it has one)". No page covers mouse order or ties at one strata and level. Wiki `API_Frame_SetFrameStrata` only says "Sets the layering strata of the frame." and links `Frame_Strata` |
| LY13 `GetWidth`, `GetHeight` and `GetSize` take `ignoreRect` (default false) | confirmed | `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleScriptRegionAPIDocumentation.lua:145` (`GetHeight`), `:260` (`GetSize`), `:307` (`GetWidth`): `{ Name = "ignoreRect", Type = "bool", Nilable = false, Default = false }`; `classic_era` `:135`, `:226`, `:268` (same three functions); `api/retail/SimpleScriptRegionAPI.lua:152, 303, 362` (`name = "ignoreRect"`). No page says what `ignoreRect` changes |
| LY14 `SetClampedToScreen(true)` keeps the frame on the screen whenever its rect resolves, not only during a drag: the whole rect moves the least distance that puts its clamp rect inside the screen. The clamp rect is the rect with each edge moved by `SetClampRectInsets(left, right, top, bottom)` (each inset is added to its edge's coordinate: x for left and right, y for top and bottom); a clamp rect wider or taller than the screen keeps its left or top edge on screen | confirmed (clamping applies at layout time, the argument order, the signs); assumed (least-distance move of the whole rect, the oversize case) | wiki `API_Frame_SetClampedToScreen`: "Prevents the frame from moving off-screen." and "This method prevents the frame from being moved partially off-screen by the following methods: Any calls to Region:SetPoint(), User dragging after Frame:StartMoving(), User resizing after Frame:StartSizing()"; its example: `SpellBookFrame:SetClampedToScreen(true)` then `SetPoint("TOPLEFT", UIParent, "TOPLEFT", -100, 0)`, "Despite calling SetPoint() toward 100 pixels beyond the left screen edge, the SpellBookFrame remains fully visible on the screen". `Blizzard_Menu/Menu.lua:1525-1530`: "After any menu position has been inverted, finish by clamping to screen. If this is a new menu, the menu will not be clamped to screen before this call." then `menuFrame:SetClampedToScreen(true);` (and `:2178` `proxy:SetClampedToScreen(false);` after "Menu is initially unclamped until after we've calculated any overflow"). Wiki `API_Frame_SetClampRectInsets`: left "positive values allow the frame to be moved off-screen" and "negative values enforce minimum distance to the edge"; right and top the reverse ("negative values allow the frame to be moved off-screen", "positive values enforce minimum distance to the edge"); bottom like left; example `f:SetClampRectInsets(42, -42, -42, 42)` "can be moved 42 size units off any edge of the screen" and "can never be entirely off-screen". Added to each edge's coordinate, those signs shrink the clamp rect for `(42, -42, -42, 42)` and grow it for `(-35, 35, 38, -50)`, as the plan reads them. Argument order `SimpleFrameAPIDocumentation.lua:1192` (`left`, `right`, `top`, `bottom`). No source states the least-distance rule or the oversize case |
| LY15 `SetPoint` takes a `point` and `relativePoint` from `FRAMEPOINT` in any case, and `SetFrameStrata` a strata from `FRAMESTRATA` in any case (stored upper case; `PARENT` inherits). A string outside the list raises at the caller (`SetPoint: 'BOGUS' is not a FRAMEPOINT`, `SetFrameStrata: 'BOGUS' is not a FRAMESTRATA`, `WORLD` included) and changes nothing; a non-string is left as before. An XML `frameStrata` outside the list is reported (`frameStrata 'BOGUS' is not a FRAMESTRATA; ignored`) and skipped, so the rest of the frame still builds | confirmed (the lists; any case for points); assumed (any case for strata, the raise, the XML report and their text: no Lua shows the C side) | retail `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:4-16` (`FRAMEPOINT`) and `:18-30` (`FRAMESTRATA`, with `BLIZZARD`); tbc 70577 `UI.xsd` adds `PARENT`; retail `blizzard_gluexml/mainline/characterservices.lua:501` calls `SetPoint("Left", ...)`. Blizzard Lua passes only list names as literals (retail 70575: every `SetFrameStrata("...")` and `SetPoint("...")` literal) |

## Render

Wiki pages read 2026-10-24 (`warcraft.wiki.gg`, raw wikitext; `Draw_layer` redirects to `Layer`, and `API_Frame_SetFrameLevel`, `API_Frame_CreateTexture`, `API_Frame_CreateFontString`, `API_Frame_SetClipsChildren`, `API_Slider_GetOrientation`, `API_StatusBar_GetOrientation` redirect to their `API:` pages). The local mirror holds only API documentation files and `Blizzard_Menu`, so the Blizzard Lua rows (`Backdrop.lua`, `NineSlice.lua`) were read from `Gethe/wow-ui-source` branch `live` at commit `15881d5` (2026-10-23) through `raw.githubusercontent.com`; their line numbers are in that copy. Paths starting `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/` are relative to `mirror/gethe/live/Interface/AddOns/` and carry the same line numbers in the mirror. No Blizzard Lua or API documentation names a draw order, so every ordering row rests on the wiki.

| Rule | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|
| V1 strata order, low to high: BACKGROUND, LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH, DIALOG, FULLSCREEN, FULLSCREEN_DIALOG, TOOLTIP | confirmed (the eight assignable strata in this order); the wiki list also starts with `WORLD`, below BACKGROUND, which the plan's list omits | wiki `Frame_Strata`: "These strata divide the z-axis into nine intervals (from back to front):" then `WORLD`, `BACKGROUND`, `LOW`, `MEDIUM`, `HIGH`, `DIALOG`, `FULLSCREEN`, `FULLSCREEN_DIALOG`, `TOOLTIP`; "WORLD is reserved for the world frame and cannot be assigned." (same list and caveat as LY12). `WORLD` is not assignable, so the kit's strata set stays the eight; no `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated` file defines the `FrameStrata` enum |
| V2 within one strata, a higher frame level draws above a lower one | confirmed | wiki `Frame_Strata`: "Frame levels further divide each strata into smaller intervals, numbered 0-10000.  Higher numbers appear above lower ones, but only if they share the same strata."; wiki `API:Frame_SetFrameLevel` (`API_Frame_SetFrameLevel`): "Frames with higher frame level are layered "in front of" frames with a lower frame level." and "By default, every frame is drawn just in front of its parent (if it has one); so usually `child:GetFrameLevel() == parent:GetFrameLevel() + 1`" |
| V3 equal strata and level: the frame created later draws above (the tie-break) | assumed (no source states the tie-break) | no source: wiki `Frame_Strata` example makes `child1` and `child2` under one parent, both at level 2 (`print(...) -- 1, 2, 2`), and says nothing about which draws on top; wiki `API:Frame_SetFrameLevel` has no sentence on equal levels; its patch note (7.0.3, TheDanW) says levels "always sort correctly using that frame level", which does not cover ties |
| V4 a frame's regions draw with that frame: above its own backdrop, below child frames of a higher level | confirmed (a frame's regions are part of that frame, so a child frame at a higher level draws over them); assumed (the order against a child frame); the backdrop is regions, not a layer under all of them: its `Center` texture is in BACKGROUND and its edge and corner pieces default to BORDER, so a region in either layer competes with them under V5-V7 and only an ARTWORK-or-higher region is always above; the snapshot exports a `center` (BACKGROUND) and an `edge` (BORDER) backdrop item per backdrop frame, each only when its file (`bgFile`, `edgeFile`) is set, sublevel 0, so they sort with those regions (the kit draws no nine-slice art, one box per piece) | wiki `Layer`: "layers are part of each frame" and "When one frame is in front of another (according to its Frame Strata), all of its LayeredRegions will also be in front"; frame level ordering as V2. `Blizzard_SharedXML/Backdrop.lua:326`: `Center = { layer = "BACKGROUND", x = x, y = y, x1 = x1, y1 = y1 },` inside `BackdropTemplateMixin:ApplyBackdrop`, applied with `NineSliceUtil.ApplyLayout(self, layout);` (`:329`); `Blizzard_SharedXML/NineSlice.lua:21`: "layer: texture draw layer, defaults to "BORDER"" and `:168`: `local layer = container.layoutTextureLayer or pieceLayout.layer or "BORDER";` then `piece:SetDrawLayer(layer, subLevel);` (`:170`). No page states a region-versus-child-frame order in so many words |
| V5 region layers, low to high: BACKGROUND, BORDER, ARTWORK, OVERLAY, HIGHLIGHT | confirmed | wiki `Layer`: "There are five named layers, in the order they are drawn (back to front):" then `BACKGROUND`, `BORDER`, `ARTWORK`, `OVERLAY`, `HIGHLIGHT (see details)` |
| V6 sublevel runs -8 to 7, default 0; lower draws first inside a layer | confirmed (range, default, direction) for textures; the wiki says a FontString ignores sublevels and draws above the textures of its layer, which the plan's rule does not say | wiki `Layer`: "Two widgets in the same layer may be further ordered using a sublayer ... specified numerically from -8 to 7 (back to front)."; wiki `API:Region_SetDrawLayer`: `sublevel: number? default=0` "Integer between -8 and 7 (inclusive) from back to front, further subdividing each layer."; wiki `API:Frame_CreateTexture`: `subLevel: number? default=0 range=-8, 7` "The level of the sublayer if textures overlap."; wiki `XML/Layer`: `textureSubLevel` range -8, 7, default 0, "Further sequences overlapping Texture widgets inside the same level". FontString difference: wiki `API:Frame_CreateFontString`: "Unlike Textures, FontStrings do not support sublayers, and will always be drawn above textures sharing the same layer."; wiki `API:Region_SetDrawLayer`: "FontStrings are always drawn above other regions sharing the same layer." The plan's snapshot gives FontStrings a `sublevel` and sorts by it; `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleFrameAPIDocumentation.lua:78-87` (`CreateFontString`) takes no `subLevel`, `:131-141` (`CreateTexture`) does |
| V7 equal layer and sublevel: the region created later draws above | assumed (no source states the tie-break) | no source: wiki `Layer` says only that sublayers further order "Two widgets in the same layer" and gives no rule for equal sublayers; wiki `API:Region_SetDrawLayer`, `API:Frame_CreateTexture`, `XML/Layer` are silent on creation order. The wiki's only same-layer statement is the FontString rule under V6 |
| V8 `CreateTexture` and `CreateFontString` without a layer use ARTWORK, sublevel 0 | confirmed (`CreateFontString` layer ARTWORK; `CreateTexture` sublevel 0); assumed (`CreateTexture` layer ARTWORK) | wiki `API:Frame_CreateFontString`: `drawLayer: DrawLayer? default=ARTWORK` "The layer the font should be drawn in."; wiki `API:Frame_CreateTexture`: `subLevel: number? default=0 range=-8, 7`, `drawLayer: DrawLayer?` with "The layer the texture should be drawn in." and no default stated. The XML twin is stated: wiki `XML/Layer` `level` "Default `ARTWORK`", `textureSubLevel` "Default `0`". `Blizzard_SharedXML/NineSlice.lua:22`: "subLevel: texture sublevel, defaults to the same default as the CreateTexture API." A FontString has no sublevel (V6), so "sublevel 0" holds only for a texture |
| V9 a HIGHLIGHT-layer region draws only while the mouse is over its frame | confirmed (the wiki ties it to mouse input being enabled on the frame) | wiki `Layer`, Details: "Setting Frame:EnableMouse() causes HIGHLIGHT to show/hide as the cursor hovers the Frame." The page does not say what a frame without mouse enabled does with a HIGHLIGHT region; the plan's rule reads that as never drawn |
| V10 `SetClipsChildren(true)` clips every descendant (frames and regions) to the frame's rect; a ScrollFrame always clips its scroll child; nested clips intersect | confirmed (clipping of overflowing children exists and is off by default); assumed (descendants and regions, the frame's rect, ScrollFrame, nesting) | wiki `XML/Frame`, attribute `clipChildren` (default `false`, Lua equivalent `Frame:SetClipsChildren`): "Clips overflowing children"; wiki `API:Frame_SetClipsChildren`: "Enabling clipping also enables render layer flattening for this frame."; `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleFrameAPIDocumentation.lua:1217` (`SetClipsChildren`, `clipsChildren: bool`) and `:188` (`DoesClipChildren`). No source says what a ScrollFrame clips: wiki `UIOBJECT_ScrollFrame` and `XML/ScrollFrame` say only "provides a scroll bar for the user to scroll a <ScrollChild> frame", and `API:ScrollFrame_SetScrollChild` is "Sets the scroll child for the scroll frame." (`SimpleScrollFrameAPIDocumentation.lua:91`). Blizzard calls `SetClipsChildren(true)` on a slide-open frame (`Blizzard_ObjectiveTracker/Blizzard_ObjectiveTrackerShared.lua:404-406`) but states no rule |
| V11 a fresh FontString: JustifyH CENTER, JustifyV MIDDLE, word wrap on, max lines 0, spacing 0 | confirmed (the XML attribute defaults); assumed (that a `CreateFontString` object with no XML has the same) | wiki `XML/FontString`, attribute table, Default column: `spacing` `0.0` ("The distance between lines"), `wordwrap` `true`, `justifyV` `MIDDLE`, `justifyH` `CENTER`, `maxLines` `0`. The Lua pages state no default: wiki `API:FontString_SetJustifyH`, `API:FontString_SetJustifyV`, `API:FontString_SetSpacing`, `API:FontString_SetMaxLines`, `API:FontString_SetWordWrap` and their getters list only the argument types; `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleFontStringAPIDocumentation.lua` has no `Default` on those arguments |
| V12 a fresh Slider's orientation is VERTICAL; a fresh StatusBar's is HORIZONTAL | confirmed | wiki `API:Slider_GetOrientation`: "`VERTICAL` - Slider thumb moves from top to bottom as the slider's value increases (default)."; wiki `API:Slider_SetOrientation`: `orientation: Orientation default=VERTICAL`; wiki `XML/Slider`: `orientation` default `VERTICAL`. Wiki `API:StatusBar_SetOrientation`: `orientation: Orientation default=HORIZONTAL`, "`HORIZONTAL` - Fills from left to right as the status bar value increases."; wiki `XML/StatusBar`: `orientation` default `HORIZONTAL`. `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleSliderAPIDocumentation.lua:53` and `SimpleStatusBarAPIDocumentation.lua:53` (`GetOrientation`, `Orientation`, non-nilable) state no default |
| V13 `BackdropTemplateMixin`: colours unset read as 1, 1, 1, 1 and `SetBackdropColor`/`SetBackdropBorderColor` alpha defaults to 1 | confirmed (once a backdrop is set; before it `GetBackdropColor` and `GetBackdropBorderColor` return nothing) | `Blizzard_SharedXML/Backdrop.lua:330-331` in `BackdropTemplateMixin:ApplyBackdrop`, called by `SetBackdrop` (`:346-347`): `self:SetBackdropColor(1, 1, 1, 1);` and `self:SetBackdropBorderColor(1, 1, 1, 1);`. Alpha: `SetBackdropColor` `self.Center:SetVertexColor(r, g, b, a or 1);` (`:412`), `SetBackdropBorderColor` `region:SetVertexColor(r, g, b, a or 1);` (`:437`). Without a backdrop: `if not self.backdropInfo then return; end` (`:398-400`, `:407-410`, `:417-419`, `:430-433`). The `OnBackdropLoaded` path (XML `backdropInfo`) uses `local r, g, b = 1, 1, 1;` and `self.backdropColorAlpha or 1` (`:168-173`, `:176-181`) |
| V14 `number \|4singular:plural;` shows the form the number picks: the singular when the last number shown ahead of the escape on its line is 1, else the plural (also with no number); `\|4` without its `;` is not an escape and shows as typed. The text keeps the raw escape (`GetText`, `format(DAYS_ABBR, n)`); the font string draws the form and is measured with it | confirmed (the escape and its two forms, invalid escapes shown as typed); assumed (which number picks, singular only for 1, plural with no number; the ruRU third form is not chosen) | wiki `UI_escape_sequences` (read 2026-10-29): `\|4` "This assigns the singular or plural form depending on the number.", examples "1 \|4car:cars;" gives "1 car", "2 \|4car:cars;" gives "2 cars"; ruRU form `\|4singular:plural1:plural2;`; "Any invalid escape sequences will be displayed as-is or is otherwise undefined behavior." Strings: extract retail 70575 and tbc 70577 `strings/enUS.lua` `DAYS_ABBR = "%d \|4Day:Days;"`; retail `blizzard_mailframe/mailframe.lua:266` builds `GREEN_FONT_COLOR_CODE..format(DAYS_ABBR, floor(daysLeft)).." "..FONT_COLOR_CODE_CLOSE`, so `format` leaves the escape in the text. `ui/measure.lua` `pluralForm`, `viewer/viewer_text.js` `pluralForm`; tests `test_a_plural_escape_shows_the_form_its_number_picks` (`tests/ui/test_measure.lua`), `tests/viewer/unit_text.js` |
| V15 a FontString whose rect is shorter than one line still draws that line, centred on the rect by its JustifyV like any overflow | assumed (no page states it; Blizzard ships such a FontString for text the auction house shows) | no source states the rule. Extract retail 70575 `blizzard_auctionhouseui/shared/blizzard_auctionhousetablebuilder.xml:143-149`: the item name cell's `Text` FontString has `<Size x="0" y="1"/>` and only LEFT and RIGHT anchors, and `blizzard_auctionhousetablebuilder.lua:704-712` sets the item name into it. `viewer/viewer_font.js` gives the lines box a minimum height of one line; test `fontString draws one line in a rect shorter than the line` (`tests/viewer/unit_text.js`) |

## Client extract

`python -m tools.client extract` reads the installed client and writes Blizzard's Interface code under
`$TESTKIT_HOME/client/<flavor>/<build>/code/` (lowercase paths, minus the leading `interface/`) and its GlobalStrings
under `strings/<locale>.lua`. The extract is local-only: it is never committed, and it holds Blizzard's files, so a
reader of this repo cannot open it.

A row may cite it as a second source beside the Gethe mirror: `Blizzard FrameXML, local extract <flavor> build <n>,
code/<path>:<line>`. A row whose only source is the extract says so, because only the mirror's lines can be checked from
the repo. A rule stays `assumed` (test name ending `_assumed`) until Blizzard's code sets it or plainly relies on it;
many widget defaults live in the C++ engine, so a search of the extract that finds nothing is recorded as "still
unconfirmed: searched X, found nothing", not as a confirmation. No Blizzard text is quoted beyond one identifier per
citation.

Slice 2 defaults checked against `Blizzard FrameXML, local extract retail build 70575` (all paths under `code/addons/`):

| Default | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|
| EditBox cursor starts at 0 | still unconfirmed | Searched every `GetCursorPosition`/`SetCursorPosition` use and `blizzard_apidocumentationgenerated/simpleeditboxapidocumentation.lua` (non-nilable return, no default); found nothing that states or relies on a fresh box's value. Hint only: code resets with `SetCursorPosition(0)` right after `SetText` (`blizzard_calendar/mainline/blizzard_calendar.lua:3528`, `blizzard_photosharing/blizzard_photosharing.lua:23`), so `SetText` may not leave the cursor at 0 |
| StatusBar colour starts white | still unconfirmed | Searched `GetStatusBarColor` uses (`blizzard_unitframe/shared/compactunitframe.lua:698` reads the old colour, no value assumed), `simplestatusbarapidocumentation.lua` (non-nilable, no default) and `blizzard_sharedxml/ui.xsd` (`BarColor` is `ColorType`, whose attributes default to 0, not a bar's initial colour); found nothing |
| StatusBar min/max start at 0, 0 | still unconfirmed | Searched `GetMinMaxValues` uses; `blizzard_gametooltip/healthbar.lua:7-14` guards `max - min > 0` but is consistent with any range; `ui.xsd` `minValue`/`maxValue`/`defaultValue` carry no default; found nothing |
| ScrollingMessageFrame message without colour is white | confirmed for a chat frame; still unconfirmed for a bare frame | `blizzard_sharedxml/scrollingmessageframe.lua:644` (`messageInfo.r or fontR`, from `:634` `self:GetTextColor()`), `blizzard_sharedxml/fontableframemixin.lua:38-41` (`GetTextColor` reads the font object), `blizzard_chatframebase/mainline/chatframeoverrides.lua:4` (`SetFontObject(ChatFontNormal)`), `blizzard_fonts_shared/shared/fonts.xml:1373-1374` (`ChatFontNormal` colour 1, 1, 1). A frame with no font object returns nothing from `GetTextColor`: the engine decides |
| nested `|c` colours: what `|r` returns to | still unconfirmed | Searched `|c`/`|r` literals, `FONT_COLOR_CODE_CLOSE` chains and comments on colour escapes; every use is a flat `|c...|r` pair or re-opens a colour after `|r` (`blizzard_uipanels_game/mainline/paperdollframe.lua:628`), none depends on a pop. The kit's `snapshot.lua` pops one level per `|r` |

## XML

Wiki pages read 2026-10-25 (`warcraft.wiki.gg`, raw wikitext; `XML_schema` redirects to `XML`, `XML/Anchors` and `XML/Layers` to `XML/Anchor` and `XML/Layer`; `XML/Size` and `XML/Animations` are 404, so `<Size>` is read from `XML/Dimension` and `XML/LayoutFrame` and `<Animations>` from `XML/LayoutFrame` and `XML/AnimationGroup`). A Blizzard XML path is relative to `mirror/gethe/live/Interface/AddOns/` at the `live` commit above; the files under `mirror/xml/` are those paths flattened (`Interface__AddOns__` plus the path with `/` as `__`), plus `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd` and `Blizzard_GameTooltip/Mainline/GameTooltip.xml` fetched from that commit. No local client extract existed when the rows were checked, so no row cites one. A row `confirmed` in one part and `assumed` in another follows the Layout and Render sections: the test of the assumed part ends in `_assumed`.

| Rule | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|
| X1 `parent="Name"` makes the frame a child of the global `Name`; with no `parent` attribute the frame is parentless, not a child of `UIParent` (the plan expected `UIParent`); a `Name` that is missing or names no widget (a string, a plain table) is reported and the frame keeps its enclosing frame, parentless at the top level | confirmed (`parent="UIParent"` names the global frame); assumed (a frame with no `parent` is parentless; any other global name; the missing or non-widget outcome) | wiki `XML`, example "Shows a texture on the center of the screen": `<Frame parent="UIParent">`; `Blizzard_GameTooltip/Mainline/GameTooltip.xml:239-242, 249, 276, 313, 322` give every shown top-level frame `parent="UIParent"`. No page says what XML does without it. Wiki `API:CreateFrame`, `parent`: "Parent object to assign to the frame, or `nil` to be parentless", so the Lua side has no `UIParent` default; the lean is parentless, not shown to hold for XML. `Blizzard_SharedXML/SharedTooltipTemplates.xml:81` (`SharedTooltipDefaultContainer`, hidden) has no `parent`, and nothing reads its parent. Test `tests/ui/test_xml_sandbox.lua` (a string and a table global as `parent`) |
| X2 `$parent` in `name` is replaced by the parent frame's name (a region's parent is its frame); with an anonymous parent the client's result is unknown | confirmed (a named parent frame); assumed (a region's parent is its frame; an anonymous parent) | wiki `XML/LayoutFrame`, `name`: "Adds a reference to `_G`, substituting `$parent` with a parent's name", example `<Frame name="$parentChild1" inherits="ContainerChildTemplate" />  <!-- name="ContainerChild1" -->` inside `<Frame name="Container">`. Mirror: `Blizzard_FrameXML/DeprecatedTemplates.xml:49-66` names `<Texture name="$parentLeftDisabled">` and anchors `relativeTo="$parentLeftDisabled"`, consistent with a region's `$parent` being its frame, but no line states it. No page says what `$parent` gives under a parent with no name |
| X3 `parentKey="K"` sets `parent.K = child` when the child is created; `parentArray="A"` appends to `parent.A` in creation order, creating the table | confirmed (`parentKey` sets the key on the parent; `parentArray` inserts into an array on the parent); assumed (the key is set before the child's own `OnLoad`; the array table is created when absent; order is document order) | wiki `API:Object_SetParentKey`: "Sets a key on the parent to the child object." and "This is similar to the XML `parentKey` attribute."; wiki `XML/LayoutFrame`, `parentArray`: "Inserts a reference to an array in the widget's parent" (`tinsert`). Blizzard Lua reads the keys: `Blizzard_Menu/MenuTemplates.lua:688` (`self.Text:SetText`, `FontString parentKey="Text"` at `Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml:99`) and `:860` (`self.ResetButton`, `Button parentKey="ResetButton"` at `Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml:83`). No line shows the table creation or the order |
| X4 `inherits` lists templates left to right, each applied before the element's own content; the element's `<Anchors>` replace inherited ones; `<Size>` is per axis; a script replaces the inherited one unless `inherit="prepend"` or `"append"` | confirmed (`inherits` is an ordered list; `inherit` is `prepend`, `append` or `none`, default `none`, and hooks the inherited script); assumed (each template is applied before the element's own content; `none` replaces the inherited script; `<Anchors>` replace; `<Size>` merges per axis; which order `prepend` and `append` give) | wiki `XML/LayoutFrame`, `inherits`: "Inherits a comma-separated list of XML virtual templates"; wiki `XML/Scripts`, `inherit`: "Hooks (`prepend`, `append`) other scripts inherited through an XML virtual template", default `none`; `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:152-158, 485` (`SCRIPTINHERITTYPE` `prepend`, `append`, `none`; `inherit` default `none`). Blizzard's own comment, `Blizzard_SharedXML/Mainline/SharedUIPanelTemplates.xml:1520-1522`, says to inherit that template "LAST (the right-most in the list)" and, for a frame with its own mixin, to use `inherit="prepend"` for `OnLoad` and `OnEvent` if it defines them; the template itself has `<OnLoad method="OnDefaultScaleFrameLoad" inherit="prepend"/>` at `:1525`. The comment orders the list but does not say which side wins, and does not say a bare handler replaces. No page says what happens to inherited `<Anchors>` or `<Size>`. The plan expected replace confirmed; it is assumed |
| X5 frames build in document order: attributes, inherited content, `<Layers>` regions, `<Frames>` children, with `OnLoad` after all of an element's children exist (a child's `OnLoad` runs before its parent's) | confirmed (at a frame's `OnLoad` its mixin methods, its `KeyValues`, its `<Frames>` child frames and its `<Layers>` regions already exist); assumed (the order of attributes, inherited content, layers and frames; that a child's `OnLoad` runs before its parent's) | `Blizzard_Menu/Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml:66-117` (`WowStyle1FilterDropdownTemplate`: `KeyValue key="text"` at `:69`, `Button parentKey="ResetButton"` in `<Frames>` at `:83`, `FontString parentKey="Text"` in `<Layers>` at `:99`, `<OnLoad method="OnLoad"/>` at `:108`) runs `WowStyle1FilterDropdownMixin:OnLoad` (`MenuTemplates.lua:954-958`), which reads `self.text` (`:669-670`), sets `self.Text` (`:688`) and wires `self.ResetButton` (`:860`). The comment above it, `MenuTemplates.lua:855-856`: "The reset button needs to be defined/created prior to the OnLoad call." Wiki `UIHANDLER_OnLoad`: "Invoked when the object is created."; wiki `API:CreateFrame`, Details: "Fires the frame's OnLoad script, if it has one from an inherited template." Wiki `XML/Frames` says only that a child frame defaults to "one higher level". No line orders one child's `OnLoad` against its parent's, or layers against frames. Spec section 7 (`OnLoad` after children are built) is consistent with every line read |
| X6 a frame shown at creation (not `hidden`, parent visible) runs `OnShow` after `OnLoad` | confirmed (a frame defined in XML runs `OnShow` after `OnLoad` unless hidden); assumed (that a hidden parent also suppresses it) | wiki `UIHANDLER_OnShow`, Description: "However, if a frame is defined in XML then OnShow also fires after OnLoad unless hidden at the time." The same page: "The OnShow event is called when a hidden frame is about to become visible on screen." The page does not say whether "hidden at the time" means the frame or its parent |
| X7 `hidden="true"` creates the frame hidden and runs no `OnHide` | confirmed (`hidden` sets the initial visibility; a hidden frame gets no `OnShow` after `OnLoad`); assumed (no `OnHide` runs) | wiki `XML/LayoutFrame`, `hidden` (default `false`): "Controls the frame's initial visibility"; `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:547-570`; wiki `UIHANDLER_OnShow` as in X6. No page mentions `OnHide` at creation |
| X8 `<Anchor>`: omitted `relativeTo` and `relativeKey` mean the parent frame; `relativePoint` defaults to `point`; `relativeKey="$parent.Sib.Child"` walks keys from the parent; `relativeTo` takes `$parent` forms; a target built later in the same tree still resolves; a missing target is an error the client logs | confirmed (`relativePoint` default; `relativeKey` is a key path from `$parent`, one level; `relativeTo` takes `$parent` names; an anchor to a `<Frames>` child declared after the `<Layers>` region that anchors to it resolves); assumed (omitted target means the parent in XML; a `relativeKey` of two or more levels; a forward reference between two frames or two regions; the missing-target outcome) | wiki `XML/Anchor`: `relativePoint` "Point on the other widget to align against (defaults to the same as point)", `relativeKey` "Another widget to align against (sibling)", `relativeTo` "Another widget to align against (in `_G`)". `relativeKey="$parent.Arrow"` at `Blizzard_Menu/Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml:23`, `relativeTo="$parentLeft"` at `Blizzard_FrameXML/DeprecatedTemplates.xml:80` and `"$parentMiddle"` at `:87`. Forward reference: `Blizzard_SharedXML/Mainline/SharedUIPanelTemplates.xml:1385` anchors a `<Layers>` FontString to `$parent.DecrementButton`, declared in `<Frames>` at `:1433`; the template could not work if keys resolved in document order. No `relativeKey` with two keys exists in the files under `mirror/xml/`. For the omitted target the Lua rule LY1 holds (confirmed); no XML page restates it. Wiki `XML/Texture`, Notes: "If the XML texture has no anchors, the texture object will implicitly have :SetAllPoints() called on it." (not a row) |
| X9 `<Size x y>` and `<Size><AbsDimension x y/></Size>` are equal; `<Offset x y>` and `<Offset><AbsDimension x y/></Offset>` and the `x`/`y` attributes on `<Anchor>` are equal; `<RelDimension>` exists and is not modelled | confirmed | wiki `XML/Dimension`: "Added x and y attributes as an alternative to AbsDimension." (Patch 1.2.1); "<AbsDimension> defines coordinates in scaling-dependent units (same as <Dimension>)."; "<RelDimension> defines coordinates relative to the entire screen, where 0 and 1 are the edges."; "<Anchor> also has its own x and y attributes, so this could be written in fewer lines without <Offset> and <AbsDimension>." `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:248-267` (`Dimension`: `x`, `y`, or one `AbsDimension` or `RelDimension`), `:588-600` (`Anchor` has `Offset` of type `Dimension`, and `x`, `y`) |
| X10 `<Layer level>` default `ARTWORK`; `textureSubLevel` default 0; regions in a layer get that draw layer | confirmed | wiki `XML/Layer`, table: `level` default `ARTWORK`, `textureSubLevel` default `0`, range -8 to 7; `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:899-907` (`level` default `ARTWORK`; `textureSubLevel` default `0`, `minInclusive` -8, `maxInclusive` 7). Matches V6 and the XML half of V8; this row says nothing about `CreateTexture` |
| X11 `<KeyValue key value type>`: `type` is `string` (default), `number`, `boolean` or `global`; applied when the frame is created, before `OnLoad` | confirmed (`type` default `string`; `number`, `boolean`, `nil`, `global`, `local` exist; a `KeyValue` is set before `OnLoad`; the wiki's conversions below); assumed (what `global` and `local` store; the order against mixins and children) | `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:99-107` (`KEYVALUETYPE`: `nil`, `boolean`, `number`, `string`, `global`, `local`; the plan listed four), `:336-342` (`type` and `keyType`, default `string`); wiki `XML/KeyValues`, example comments: `boolean` "0" is false, "on" is true; `number` "two" is 0; `nil` gives nil. Timing: `KeyValue key="text" type="global" value="FILTER"` at `Blizzard_Menu/Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml:69` is read by `DropdownTextMixin:OnLoad` (`MenuTemplates.lua:668-670`) from `WowStyle1FilterDropdownMixin:OnLoad` (`:954-957`). The pages do not say what `global` stores; `FILTER` reads as a GlobalString name |
| X12 script body is the handler's function body with `self` first and the handler's argument names after it; `function="Name"` is the global of that name looked up when the element is built; `method="Name"` calls `self:Name(...)` when the handler fires | confirmed (`self`; `button` and `down` in `OnClick`; `value` in `OnValueChanged`; `function=` names a function and `method=` a widget method); assumed (argument names of every other handler; when `function=` is looked up; that `method=` passes the handler's arguments) | `Blizzard_FrameXML/SecureTemplates.xml:16`: `<OnClick> if not InCombatLockdown() then SecureActionButton_OnClick(self, button, down); end </OnClick>`; `Blizzard_GameTooltip/Mainline/GameTooltip.xml:19`: `HealthBar_OnValueChanged(self, value);` inside `<OnValueChanged>`. Wiki `XML/Scripts`: `function` "Name of a function instead of Lua code between the XML tags", `method` "Name of a widget method instead of Lua code between the XML tags"; `<OnLoad inherit="prepend" function="GameTooltip_OnLoad"/>` at `GameTooltip.xml:26`; `<OnLoad method="OnLoad"/>` at `Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml:108` resolves to a method only the mixin defines (`MenuTemplates.lua:954`). The names `button`, `down`, `value` are Blizzard's; wiki `Widget_script_handlers` lists signatures but no page says the XML body gets them as locals |
| X13 `mixin` / `secureMixin` copy each named global table onto the frame when it is created, before `KeyValues` and `OnLoad`; a missing global is an error in the client | confirmed (the attributes take comma-separated lists of Lua mixins; the mixin methods exist at `OnLoad`); assumed (the copy happens before `KeyValues`; the effect of `secureMixin`; a missing global) | wiki `XML/LayoutFrame`: `mixin` "Inherits a comma-separated list of Lua mixins", `secureMixin` "Inherits a comma-separated list of Lua mixins, wrapping inherited functions to force secure execution". `Blizzard_GameTooltip/Mainline/GameTooltip.xml:9` uses both on one element. `<OnLoad method="OnLoad"/>` (`Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml:108`) runs `WowStyle1FilterDropdownMixin:OnLoad` (`MenuTemplates.lua:954`), so the mixin is on the frame by then. Nothing orders mixin against `KeyValues`, and nothing names a missing global. The 12.1.0 `<Mixins><Mixin key source/>` form (`UI.xsd:360-366`, wiki `XML/Mixins`) is not modelled |
| X14 `virtual="true"` registers a template and builds nothing; a later definition of the same name replaces the earlier; only non-virtual elements make `_G[name]` | confirmed (virtual builds a template, not a widget, so no frame and no `_G[name]`); assumed (a later definition replaces the earlier) | wiki `XML/LayoutFrame`, `virtual` (default `false`): "Creates an XML virtual template instead of a widget (requires name)"; `name`: "Adds a reference to `_G`". No virtual name occurs twice in the files under `mirror/xml/`, and no page covers a repeated name |
| X15 `<Script file>` and `<Include file>` paths are relative to the including XML file's folder; inline `<Script>` text runs at load in document order | confirmed (document order; inline `<Script>` runs when reached); assumed (the base folder of `file=`; the plan expected this confirmed) | wiki `XML/Script`, Details: "Executed when reached by the XML parser, going from top to bottom.", with the example that a `<Script>` after `<Frame name="foo">` can read `foo.bar` and one before it errors; wiki `XML/Include`: same sentence. Both pages say only `file`: "Relative path". Wiki `XML/Include`: "If called from within a subdirectory (`MyAddOn\libs\SomeLib.xml`) then relative pathnames function only when the .toc uses backslashes" leans to the including file's folder. The two Blizzard lines, `Blizzard_SharedXML/Backdrop.xml:3` (`<Script file="Backdrop.lua"/>`) and `Blizzard_FrameXML/DeprecatedTemplates.xml:3`, name a `.lua` beside an XML at the addon root, which fits both readings. `ui/xml.lua` (`join`, used by `walk` and `include`) uses the including file's folder. Wiki `XML/Script` also says `file` takes TOC inline directives and variables, which `ui/xml.lua` does not read |
| X16 tags and attribute names are matched case-insensitively; an element the client does not know is an error | assumed | no source: the files under `mirror/xml/` spell every tag in its documented case (415 `<Anchors`, 109 `<Layers`, 121 `<Scripts`, 50 `<Frames`; no lowercase-initial tag); no wiki `XML` page mentions case or unknown elements (wiki `UIHANDLER_OnLoad` has an example closing `</Onload>`, a typo, not a source). The kit is lenient on case and reports an unknown tag instead of failing |
| X17 Texture `file` / `atlas` / `useAtlasSize` / `alphaMode` call `SetTexture` / `SetAtlas(atlas, useAtlasSize)` / `SetBlendMode` | confirmed (`file` is `SetTexture`; `atlas` and `useAtlasSize` are `SetAtlas(atlas, useAtlasSize)`; `alphaMode` takes `DISABLE`, `BLEND`, `ALPHAKEY`, `ADD`, `MOD`, default `BLEND`); assumed (`alphaMode` is `SetBlendMode`; the plan expected this confirmed) | wiki `XML/Texture`, "Lua Equivalent" column: `file` `TextureBase:SetTexture`, `atlas` and `useAtlasSize` `TextureBase:SetAtlas`; the `alphaMode` row has default `BLEND` and an empty Lua-equivalent cell. Wiki `API:TextureBase_SetAtlas`: `TextureBase:SetAtlas(atlas [, useAtlasSize [, ...]])`. `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:42-50, 632` (`ALPHAMODE`, default `BLEND`); wiki `API:TextureBase_SetBlendMode`: `blendMode`: `DISABLE, BLEND, ALPHAKEY, ADD, MOD`, the same five values |
| X18 `<Color r g b a>` in a Texture is `SetColorTexture`, in a FontString `SetTextColor`; `a` defaults to 1; `<TexCoords left right top bottom>` is `SetTexCoord(left, right, top, bottom)` | confirmed (`a` defaults to 1.0 and `r`, `g`, `b` to 0.0; `<Color>` is a child of both Texture and FontString; `TexCoords` edges are normalized from the top left; `SetTexCoord(left, right, top, bottom)`); assumed (which setter a `<Color>` calls) | wiki `XML/Color`: `a` "Alpha (opacity)" default 1.0, `r`, `g`, `b` default 0.0; `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:307-313` (`ColorType`), `:699` (Texture child), `:749` (FontString child). Wiki `XML/TexCoords`: `top` "Top edge, normalized from top (0) to bottom (1)"; wiki `API:TextureBase_SetTexCoord`: `TextureBase:SetTexCoord(left, right, top, bottom)`, `top` "Top (or minY) edge of the scaled/cropped image, as a fraction of the image's height from the top." Blizzard's own API documentation names the third and fourth arguments the other way round: `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleTextureBaseAPIDocumentation.lua:580-587` lists `left`, `right`, `bottom`, `top`; only the wiki's wording is used here. No page maps `<Color>` to `SetColorTexture` or `SetTextColor` (wiki `XML/Color` lists Texture, Font and Shadow as parents) |
| X19 FontString `text` names a global string when a global of that name is a string, else it is the literal text; `inherits` on a FontString names a font object | assumed | no source: Blizzard XML uses both forms, `text="MACRO_POPUP_CHOOSE_ICON"` (`Blizzard_SharedXML/Mainline/SharedUIPanelTemplates.xml:1664`) and `text="ICON_SELECTION_DRAG"` (`:1753`) beside the literals `text="-"` (`Blizzard_SharedXML/Shared/InputBox/InputBoxTemplates.xml:388`) and `text=""` (`SharedUIPanelTemplates.xml:584`), and `inherits="GameFontHighlightSmall"` on many FontStrings; that is consistent with the rule but no line states it. Wiki `XML/FontString`: `text` has Lua equivalent `FontString:SetText` and no further description |
| X20 `<Animations>` holds `<AnimationGroup name parentKey looping setToFinalAlpha>`; each child (`Alpha`, `Translation`, `Scale`, `Rotation`, `Animation`, ...) is an animation of that type with attributes `duration`, `startDelay`, `endDelay`, `order`, `smoothing`, `fromAlpha`/`toAlpha`, `offsetX`/`offsetY`, `scaleX`/`scaleY`, `degrees`, `childKey`/`targetKey`/`target`, `parentKey`; groups are built when the frame is built and do not play until `Play` or an `OnLoad` that calls it | confirmed (the element and attribute set); assumed (built with the frame; not played until `Play`) | `Blizzard_SharedXML/UI.xsd:608-614` (`Animations` holds `AnimationGroup`), `:1719-1738` (`AnimationGroup`: `name`, `mixin`, `inherits`, `virtual`, `parentKey`, `parentArray`, `looping` `NONE`/`REPEAT`/`BOUNCE` default `NONE`, `setToFinalAlpha` default `false`), `:1561-1586` (animation: `name`, `mixin`, `secureMixin`, `inherits`, `virtual`, `target`, `targetKey`, `parentKey`, `childKey`, `startDelay` and `endDelay` default 0.0, `duration`, `order` default 1, `smoothing` `NONE`/`IN`/`OUT`/`IN_OUT`/`OUT_IN` default `NONE`), `:1590-1646` (`Translation` `offsetX`/`offsetY`; `Rotation` `degrees`/`radians`; `Scale` `scaleX`/`scaleY` and from/to forms; `Alpha` `fromAlpha` 0.0, `toAlpha` 1.0); more types exist (`LineTranslation`, `LineScale`, `Path`, `FlipBook`, `VertexColor`, `TextureCoordTranslation`). Wiki `XML/Animation` gives `duration` default 0.0 and `order` range 1 to 100; the schema has no `duration` default and `order` 0 to 100. No attribute or sentence makes a group play on creation, and the files under `mirror/xml/` hold no Lua that calls `Play`; `Blizzard_SharedXML/Mainline/SharedUIPanelTemplates.xml:1018-1023` declares a `looping="REPEAT"` group and nothing in the XML starts it |
| X21 Texture, FontString and Line take `mixin=` and `secureMixin=` like frames (copied in order, names recorded; secure wrapping not modelled) | confirmed (extract) | Blizzard FrameXML, local extract retail build 70575, code/addons/blizzard_sharedxml/ui.xsd:547-570 (mixin :552-553); 101 region uses, e.g. AutoScalingFontStringMixin |
| X22 with `inherit="prepend"` or `"append"` both handlers run even when the first raises; the error is recorded | assumed | wiki XML/Scripts names prepend/append only (row X4); the kit isolates a script from its hooks the same way (F3) |
| X23 `<Script file>` and `<Include file>` read `file=` literally; inline `[AllowLoadGameType]` directives are a TOC feature | assumed | wiki TOC_format, "TOC files support the inline use of directives" (11.1.5); 0 of 1093 extract XML files put brackets in `file=` |
| X24 an element with `intrinsic="true"` declares a frame type named by its `name`, based on its tag, and builds no frame; `CreateFrame("<name>")` and a `<name>` tag build a base-type frame with the element's content applied first, then the template list or `inherits`, then the element's own content; its `<Scripts>` become handlers that run before the frame's own script (`intrinsicOrder="precall"`, the default) or after it and its hooks (`"postcall"`) and that `SetScript` cannot remove; `GetObjectType()` is the base type (row D8); intrinsics are per world, from whatever XML that world loaded; a real XML intrinsic replaces a kit fake of the same name | confirmed (declaration, tag and `CreateFrame` use, precall default, postcall order, not removable, `GetObjectType`); assumed (content applied before `inherits` and own content; postcall runs after the hooks; the intrinsic's `OnLoad` fires from a `CreateFrame` with no template list; replacing a kit fake) | wiki `Intrinsic_frame` (read 2026-10-28): "The `name` attribute becomes the new tag name in XML or the frame type in CreateFrame"; "These can be created through CreateFrame like built-in widgets" and "through XML elements anywhere a frame type could"; "GetObjectType returns the intrinsic's type, not the intrinsic's name"; `intrinsicOrder="precall"` "called _before_ the normal script handler", `"postcall"` "_after_", default "precall"; "Intrinsic scripts cannot be removed after being defined"; "It is not possible to declare an intrinsic based on another intrinsic." Local extract retail build 70575: `blizzard_itembutton/shared/itembuttontemplate.xml:4` `<Button name="ItemButton" registerForClicks="LeftButtonUp, RightButtonUp" mixin="ItemButtonMixin" intrinsic="true">` with `<OnShow method="PostOnShow" intrinsicOrder="postcall"/>` (also `OnHide`, `OnEvent`); 12 retail XML files declare intrinsics |
| X25 a template's root `parentKey` / `parentArray` applies to every widget that inherits it (XML `inherits` or a `CreateFrame` template list), on that widget's parent; the element's own attribute wins, then the nearest template's | confirmed (an inheriting frame element in XML without its own `parentArray` is added to the template's array); assumed (the `CreateFrame` template list does the same; region templates, in XML or through `CreateTexture` / `CreateFontString`, do the same; precedence between the element, nested templates and a list) | local extract retail build 70575, `blizzard_mailframe/mailframe.xml:173` `<Button name="SendMailAttachment" parentArray="SendMailAttachments" virtual="true">`, used as `<Button name="SendMailAttachment1" inherits="SendMailAttachment" id="1"/>` (`:697-700`, no `parentArray` of their own), and `mailframe.lua:965` reads `SendMailFrame.SendMailAttachments[i]` |
| X26 a `<KeyValue type="global">` value is a dotted path: the first part is a global and each later part a field of the table before it, so `MoneyDenominationDisplayType.Copper` stores that table's `Copper`; a missing or non-table link stores nil without an error | confirmed (278 retail 70575 XML files use `type="global"`, and the code reads the stored value as the enum member: `MoneyDenominationDisplayMixin:OnLoad` raises when `displayType` is nil); assumed (a missing link gives nil, not an error) | extract retail 70575 `blizzard_moneyframe/shared/moneyframe.xml:7,54-64` (`value="MoneyDenominationDisplayType.Copper"`), `blizzard_moneyframe/shared/moneyframe.lua:368-371`, `blizzard_auctionhouseui/shared/blizzard_auctionhouseframe.xml:46,54` (`AuctionHouseFrameDisplayMode.Buy`), read at `blizzard_auctionhouseui/shared/blizzard_auctionhouseframe.lua:378`; test `tests/ui/test_xml_keyvalues.lua`. Only `KeyValue` stores a global's value: `mixin`, `parent` and `relativeTo` name one global and stay flat; `<Attribute>` is not modelled (BU17) |
| X27 an `<Anchor>` `point` or `relativePoint` outside `FRAMEPOINT`, or a `frameStrata` outside `FRAMESTRATA`, is reported at the element's line and ignored: the anchor is skipped, the frame keeps its strata, and the element and its children still build; the value's case is not checked | confirmed (the two attribute types and their value lists); assumed (the outcome for another value; that case does not matter) | extract retail 70575 `blizzard_sharedxml/ui.xsd:4-16` (`FRAMEPOINT`: `TOPLEFT`, `TOPRIGHT`, `BOTTOMLEFT`, `BOTTOMRIGHT`, `TOP`, `BOTTOM`, `LEFT`, `RIGHT`, `CENTER`), `:18-30` (`FRAMESTRATA`: `BACKGROUND`, `LOW`, `MEDIUM`, `HIGH`, `DIALOG`, `FULLSCREEN`, `FULLSCREEN_DIALOG`, `TOOLTIP`, `BLIZZARD`), `:593, :596` (`<Anchor>` `point` and `relativePoint` are `FRAMEPOINT`), `:836` (`frameStrata` is `FRAMESTRATA`). Every `frameStrata` and `<Anchor>` point in the retail and tbc extract XML is one of these values in upper case. The client's XML loader is C; no page says what it does with another value (FrameXML.log is the likely outlet). Test `tests/test_addon_xml.lua`; `frameStrata="PARENT"` (tbc 70577 `blizzard_sharedxml/ui.xsd:18-31` lists it and makes it the default) means inherit and is not reported, assumed the same on retail, whose ui.xsd leaves it out |

## Widgets

Wiki pages read 2026-10-26 (`warcraft.wiki.gg`, fetched through a summarising reader, so a quoted sentence is secondhand and a page that 404s is `assumed`); signatures from `api/<flavor>/`; Blizzard citations are `path:line` in the local client extract (retail build 70575, `code/addons/<path>`), and a classic_era file is cited as `Gethe/wow-ui-source 8165d4c` (pinned classic_era commit); the repo was at `e28e465`. The Phase 6 plan's bracketed expectations were checked, not copied: where a source disagrees the row states what the source says. `xsd` is `blizzard_sharedxml/ui.xsd`.

| Rule | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|
| W1 `SetTexture`, `SetColorTexture` and `SetAtlas` each replace the texture's source: the last call wins and the earlier source no longer reads back | assumed | no source: wiki `API_TextureBase_SetTexture` says only "To clear the texture call this without any params or nil"; no wiki page or Blizzard call site states how the three setters interact |
| W2 `GetTexture` returns the path or file id last set and is nil when none was; `GetAtlas` returns the atlas name last set, else nil; code reads one or the other | confirmed (callers branch on `GetAtlas()` truthy, else `GetTexture()`, and use `GetTexture() or GetAtlas()`); assumed (nil after `SetColorTexture`; the docs mark `GetAtlas` not nilable while `GetTexture` returns `textureFile:cstring?`) | `blizzard_sharedmapdataproviders/frameclonemanager.lua:113-118`, `blizzard_sharedmapdataproviders/sharedmappoitemplates.lua:244`, `blizzard_sharedtalentui/blizzard_talentdisplay.lua:225`; `api/retail/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua` `GetTexture`, `GetAtlas` |
| W3 `SetAtlas(atlas, useAtlasSize=false, filterMode?, resetTexCoords?, wrapModeHorizontal?, wrapModeVertical?)`: `useAtlasSize` true sizes the region to the atlas; `GetAtlas` returns the name | confirmed | `api/retail/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:401` (`SetAtlas` arguments and defaults); wiki `API_TextureBase_SetAtlas`: "True if the size of the texture should be changed to match the dimensions of the atlas." |
| W4 `SetTexCoord(left, right, top, bottom)` expands to eight numbers in the order UL.x, UL.y, LL.x, LL.y, UR.x, UR.y, LR.x, LR.y = `l, t, l, b, r, t, r, b`; a fresh texture reads `0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 0, 1, 1` | confirmed (the 4-argument order and the eight-value order); assumed (the fresh-texture default: no page states it, the wiki says only "(0,0) is the top-left corner of the image; (1,1) is the bottom right corner") | `blizzard_sharedxml/nineslice.lua:78` (`SetTexCoord(left, right, top, bottom)` call), `blizzard_sharedxml/backdrop.lua:215` and `blizzard_sharedxml/mixinutil.lua:68` (eight-value order UL, LL, UR, LR); `api/retail/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:162` `GetTexCoord` returns `ulX, ulY, llX, llY, urX, urY, lrX, lrY`. The api doc names the 4-argument parameters `left, right, bottom, top`, which is the opposite of the call-site order for the last two: the call site wins |
| W5 eight arguments to `SetTexCoord` are stored as given, and `GetTexCoord` returns them back | confirmed | `blizzard_sharedmapdataproviders/frameclonemanager.lua:117` (`clone:SetTexCoord(original:GetTexCoord())` round trip); `api/retail/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:765` `SetTexCoord` |
| W6 vertex colour `SetVertexColor(r, g, b, a)`: alpha defaults 1; a fresh texture reads `1, 1, 1, 1` | confirmed (alpha default); assumed (the fresh colour) | wiki `API_Region_SetVertexColor`: "number? = 1.0 [0.0 - 1.0] - Alpha (opacity)"; no page states the default colour |
| W7 blend mode values `DISABLE BLEND ALPHAKEY ADD MOD`; texture default `BLEND` | confirmed | `xsd:42-48` (`ALPHAMODE`: DISABLE, BLEND, ALPHAKEY, ADD, MOD), `xsd:632` (Texture `alphaMode` default `BLEND`); `api/retail/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:451` `SetBlendMode` |
| W8 desaturation defaults false | confirmed | `xsd:648` (Texture `desaturated` default `false`); wiki `API_Texture_SetDesaturated`: "desaturated boolean? `= false`" |
| W9 `SetRotation(radians, normalizedRotationPoint)`: rotation defaults 0, rotates counter-clockwise, point defaults to the centre `{x=0.5, y=0.5}` | confirmed (default 0); assumed (the default point and direction, from the wiki summary only) | `xsd:635` (Texture `rotation` default `0.0`); wiki `API_TextureBase_SetRotation`: "Positive values rotate the texture counter-clockwise"; `api/retail/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:666` |
| W10 `SetHorizTile` and `SetVertTile` default false | confirmed | `xsd:644-645` (`horizTile`, `vertTile` default `false`); wiki `API_Texture_SetHorizTile`: "tiling boolean? `= false`" |
| W11 `SetGradient(orientation, minColor, maxColor)` takes colour objects on every flavor, including Classic Era (the plan expected `r1, g1, b1, r2, g2, b2` there); the separate-number form is gone from the docs | confirmed | `api/retail/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:541`, `api/forever/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:541`, `api/vanilla/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:453`, `api/mists/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:453`, `api/tbc/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:453` all declare `SetGradient` as `(orientation, minColor, maxColor)`; wiki `API_TextureBase_SetGradient`, Patch 10.0.0: "Changed args to ColorMixin; merged Texture:SetGradientAlpha" |
| W12 `SetColorTexture(r, g, b, a = 1)` | confirmed | `api/retail/SimpleTextureBaseAPI.lua:480` (`a:SingleColorValue?`); wiki `API_TextureBase_SetColorTexture`: "a number? `= 1`" |
| W13 `SetIgnoreParentAlpha(true)` stops the alpha product at the region and `SetIgnoreParentScale(true)` stops the effective scale; both default false | confirmed (the flag and its default); assumed (the exact stop point of the product) | wiki `API_Region_SetIgnoreParentAlpha`: "Sets whether the region should ignore its parent's alpha."; `xsd:640-641` (`ignoreParentAlpha`, `ignoreParentScale` default `false`) |
| W14 `SetDrawLayer(layer, sublevel = 0)`; sublevel range -8..7, out-of-range values clamp | confirmed (range and default); assumed (clamping) | wiki `API_Region_SetDrawLayer`: "Integer between -8 and 7 (inclusive)", sublevel "= 0"; no source says what happens outside the range |
| W15 `AddMaskTexture` is idempotent, `GetNumMaskTextures` counts the masks, masks are not drawn | assumed | no source: wiki `API_Texture_AddMaskTexture` has only the signature (variants 404); nothing in the extract states idempotence |
| W16 `GetButtonState` defaults `"NORMAL"`; `SetButtonState(state, lock=false)` takes `DISABLED`, `NORMAL` or `PUSHED` (the plan expected only NORMAL and PUSHED); Blizzard Lua passes only NORMAL and PUSHED | confirmed (the three values, `lock`, and the call sites); assumed (the default state) | wiki `API_Button_SetButtonState`: allowed values "DISABLED, NORMAL, PUSHED", signature `(buttonState [, lock])`; `api/retail/SimpleButtonAPI.lua:291` (`lock:bool=false`); `blizzard_actionbar/shared/actionbutton.lua:143,158` |
| W17 shown state texture: normal when NORMAL and enabled; pushed when PUSHED (normal if none); disabled when disabled (normal if none); highlight while the mouse is over an enabled button or after `LockHighlight`. One texture set for two roles draws while either shows, and clearing one role leaves it in the other | assumed | no source: wiki `API_Button_SetHighlightTexture` exists (signature `(asset, blendMode?)`) but `API_Button_LockHighlight` 404s and no page states the fall-back to the normal texture; `api/retail/SimpleButtonAPI.lua:432` `SetHighlightTexture`, `api/retail/SimpleFrameAPI.lua:1208` `LockHighlight` give signatures only |
| W18 `SetNormalTexture(path or Texture)` makes or adopts a region of the button that fills it (a texture object with no anchors of its own gets `SetAllPoints`; one with anchors keeps them); `GetNormalTexture` is nil when unset; a path or atlas retextures the region already filling the role, adopted or made | confirmed (adoption reparents; Blizzard relies on nil; Blizzard writes anchorless state textures); assumed (the filling anchors; the retexturing) | wiki `API_Button_SetNormalTexture`: "Although a texture object can be used as the argument, the texture will be re-parented to the button."; `blizzard_moneyframe/mainline/moneyframe.lua:152` (`GetNormalTexture() or CreateTexture()`); `blizzard_sharedxml/modelscenetemplates.xml:25-27` (`<NormalTexture file=.../>`, `<PushedTexture .../>` and `<HighlightTexture .../>` with no `<Anchors>`, so the client must place them); the docs mark `GetNormalTexture` not nilable (`api/retail/SimpleButtonAPI.lua:164`), the call site says otherwise |
| W19 normal, pushed, disabled textures draw on ARTWORK, the highlight on HIGHLIGHT; the highlight default blend is `ADD` | assumed | no source: wiki is silent on the highlight blend; of 532 `<HighlightTexture>` in the extract XML 442 write `alphaMode="ADD"` explicitly and 90 do not, so the default is not shown; `xsd:632` says a Texture defaults to `BLEND` |
| W20 `GetPushedTextOffset` default is `1, -1` | assumed | no source: `UIPanelButtonNoTooltipTemplate` (`blizzard_sharedxml/secureuipaneltemplates.xml:39`) declares no `PushedTextOffset`; `xsd:964` declares the element with no default; the extract overrides it to `0,0` 8 times, `-2,-1` twice (`blizzard_sharedxml/shared/button/threeslicebuttontemplate.xml:66,102`), `2,-2` once, `1.25,-1` once, and Lua calls `SetPushedTextOffset(0,0)` (`blizzard_achievementui/mainline/blizzard_achievementui.lua:2166`) and `SetPushedTextOffset(1, -1)` (`blizzard_sharedxml/listtemplates.lua:75`); explicit `0,0` overrides imply a nonzero default and `1, -1` is the value that fits |

| W22 shown text uses the state's font object (normal, disabled, highlight while hovered), else the normal one | assumed | no source: wiki `API_Button_SetNormalFontObject` says only "Sets the font object used for the button's normal state"; `UIPanelButtonNoTooltipTemplate` sets `GameFontNormal`, `GameFontHighlight`, `GameFontDisable` (`blizzard_sharedxml/secureuipaneltemplates.xml:82-84`) but no page states the fall-back |
